### Follow-ups from 4A
- [ ] fin-store holds the store mutex across folder I/O in `tick` (import reads, export writes): take it only around DB transactions (read/verify segments outside, apply under; write packs outside; `VACUUM INTO` on a second connection). A big follower import delays short calls today
- [ ] `ProjectStatus` (and the verify, rebuild and compact reports) still `Schema.Unknown` in contracts: generate them
- [ ] `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -D warnings` is clean (Linux, 2026-11-05) but CI does not run it: add it to CI
- [ ] Sweep orphan databases left in server data by a crash between create's DB rename and its project-file write
- [ ] `meta.createKey` is lost when a rebuild has no old DB (the key is not in the folder)
- [ ] Rational schema pattern `^(0|-?[1-9]\d*)(/[1-9]\d*)?$` accepts non-canonical `0/5` and `3/1` (the parser rejects them)
- [ ] `Probe` TS type: optional fields vs `null` mismatch
- [ ] RPC envelope check vs generated schema is a vitest test (`envelopes.test.ts`), not a `pnpm gates` gate
- [ ] Typecheck gate: project list hardcoded in `scripts/check-typecheck.mjs`; core-types tests not typechecked
- [ ] fin-node `loadNative` untested
- [ ] No e2e or RPC fixture for `tx.get` set-aside / `TX_SET_ASIDE` (unit tests only; a live server cannot produce `SALVAGED`/`SUPERSEDED` without fake clocks)
- [ ] `BASE_REV_AHEAD` vs `HISTORY_REWOUND` precision for a subscriber whose `fromRev` is beyond a rewound head
- [ ] `tx.commit.generation` and `ops.subscribe.generation` optional in protocol 1 → required in protocol 2
- [ ] Linux NFS: directory attribute cache (`acdirmax`, 60 s by default) exceeds the 10 s ordering-rule wait; NFS is classified `network` but untested. Measure, and use a longer wait or a listing refresh there
- [x] Windows (~4.9 s, not-found cache) and macOS smbfs (1.5–2.4 s): `PROJECT_NOT_FOUND` right after another host creates the project → 12 s retry on network storage, listing refresh first on macOS (7716b8a; only under an existing parent, leftover probes swept: 145d137; spec §3.13 item 14). Linux cifs/NFS take the same retry, unmeasured
- [ ] macOS smbfs: whether the lease probe can read a client-set mtime (no `AT_STATX_FORCE_SYNC`); untested
- [ ] Locked files: no test for the pending-deletion path (first sighting without metadata); a verify right after open shows `SEGMENT_LOCKED`, not `unreadable`, until the store has been open 60 s
- [ ] A segment held open by another SMB client slows compaction to one 60 s retry per file
- [x] iCloud: a first read of a dataless file can fail `ETIMEDOUT` (1 in ~25) → placeholder reads that time out are retried 3 × 1 s (`fsio::hydrating`, b23a522; not the lease's reads or the per-poll project-file reread, d022fc3/145d137); unit-tested via a hook, not re-run on iCloud
- [ ] iCloud conflict-copy names and second-device mid-upload view: need a second device
- [ ] Clean close could write a "released" lease record so other servers need not wait out 45 s of staleness
- [ ] `fin project verify` / `rebuild` / `compact` have no progress or cancellation (only `ENGINE_BUSY {operation, sinceMs}`)
- [ ] `diffs::fill` loads a whole branch's rows; restore tooling for daily backups should mint a new incarnation (backups already do; a hand-copied DB does not)
- [ ] Abandoned compaction manifests are never deleted automatically; orphan packs from a crash before the manifest stay as harmless duplicates; one manifest per branch at a time
- [ ] Conflict copies are re-read on every pass (scanner never caches foreign files); a conflicted branch directory is listed, not entered (its segments are not quarantined)
- [ ] A sync folder with losing segments re-reports the fork on every poll. Partly closed by 4B: `fin project recover --import <dir> --create all` brings a moved-out side back as a `recovered-*` branch; the fork itself still needs the manual move-out (in-band resolution: 4B follow-ups)
- [ ] A copy opened while the original's share is unmounted counts as a move; the original is then refused as the copy
- [x] Rewind rule (d) needs 4B to record branch bases (`meta` `branchBase.<b>`) — 4B Task 10 cascades instead; only hand-made roots keep the `branchBase` rule
- [x] Pack headers record replaced segments' last records (`replaces: [{hash, lastTxHash}]`, exact stand-in rule) — done in Task 13
- [x] finisherd hubs removed with their last subscriber — done in Task 17
- [ ] fin-node untested paths: `lagged` (needs > 1,024 events before registration), close on GC drop
- [ ] Warn (status) on Linux mount options that break the lease's assumptions: cifs `cache=loose`, `actimeo`/`closetimeo` > 1, SMB1 (`vers=1.0`)
- [ ] Lease: a failed in-place write of our own record could leave it torn; the holder then self-fences and the folder needs `takeover --confirm`. Consider rewriting a torn record in the heartbeat when no claim above ours exists, as `take` does after its own write
- [ ] Lease: `create_new`-only fallback for the first `lease.json` (Unix without hard links: FAT/exFAT, macOS smbfs as far as known, NAS shares refusing `link` with `EACCES`) still leaves a short record after a crash. Task 19: macOS smbfs takes that path (no hard links); Linux cifs and Windows publish atomically
- [ ] Lease: a claim `create_new` that landed but reported a transient error leaves our own orphan claim (status names it as this server's); it holds acquisition off for 45 s

- [x] Task 2: scrubbed 4A fixture (`crates/fin-store/tests/fixtures/4a`), generated from 4A code
- [x] Task 3: record kinds with typed links, content-versioned segments (edit-only v1, any 4B kind v2), per-kind reader versions, DB migration 0002 (`branches`, `record_links`)
- [x] Task 4: exact per-track shift maps (`rebase::map`: `StepMap`, `Mapping` with mirrors; ProseMirror recover rule, Decision 16)
- [x] Task 5: per-track, per-entity conflict footprints (`rebase::footprint`, one `footprint` per op module; `Conflict` with reason codes, `Conflicts` → `CONFLICT` with `details.conflicts`)
- [x] Task 6: rebase engine rewrite (`rebase_apply`: map op by op through mirrored inverses, judge unmirrored steps, re-run with full policy; `rebase_tx` in the frame at the base, `state::state_at` in commit), `clip.restore` placeholders. Proptest budget, first measure: `rebase_props` + `shift_map` 0.15 s wall in a debug build on the Linux VM (tests 0.05 s + 0.02 s; budget < 8 s). Review fixes: undo/redo mirrors from `CommittedTx.link` (`steps_from`), `Conflict.details` and re-run blame, `details.baseRev` on a base failure, commit frame by rewind or replay (`state_near_head`); unchanged timing
- [x] Task 7: one write path (`Store::write`: dedupe, read-only, fence, IMMEDIATE txn, in-memory lease re-check, commit; dry runs in a rolled-back txn with no read-only or lease check, so on followers too; IMMEDIATE after review) used by commit; lineage reads from the `branches` index (`branch::lineage`: `state_at`, `state_near_head`, `records_since` filling pending diffs per ancestor, `hash_at`/`tip_hash` with the fork-hash fallback in `refresh`); `state.rs` removed
- [x] Task 8: branch index derived from the log (`fin_core::branch`: `BranchIndex`, name rules, references `id | name | name#id`, duplicate names held by the lowest numeric id); `Store::create_branch` (`branch` record on the parent, index row, fork snapshot), `Store::set_branch` (`branchSet`: rename, archive, restore, `--restore --as`), `Store::branches`, `Store::resolve_branch`; references resolved in commit and reads; `NAME_TAKEN`, `BRANCH_ARCHIVED` (exit 3); `derived::recompute`
- [x] Task 9: branch-aware import (worklist parents first; children learnt from applied `branch` records in the same pass, with their fork snapshot; segments must start at the fork point, `Known::fork`; unfit `branch`/`branchSet` records set aside `MALFORMED`, replaced by inert rows in Task 9b), Decision 8 (a set-aside `branch` record takes its directories' segments along, recursively; the same id made on two servers across a takeover keeps the set-aside branch's segments off the live one; directories with an unknown `branch` record wait: `unknownBranches`), export parents first, counters past branch ids and quarantined ids (`Counters::raise_to_quarantine`), status `branches[]` gains `name, parent, forkSeq, state`; Task 8 review fixes (writes refuse index-less branches, absorb validates names and fork hashes, `try-<n>-2` defaults, no-op `branchSet` refused)
- [x] Task 9b: branch identity across servers (Decision 33): `branch` records carry their fence `epoch`; an id made twice goes to (epoch desc, txHash asc), the loser is an inert row (its branch's content `ID_COLLISION`, applied subtrees voided through the cascade), unfit branch records are inert rows too (`record_links.ignored`, warning `BRANCH_RECORDS_IGNORED`); every branch segment names its `branch` record (`branchRecord`, always format 2) and is classified by it; seeds stop below the first branch record
- [x] Task 10: rewinds and diverged tails cascade to descendant branches whose `branch` record was lost, recursively (`import::cascade`: rows to quarantine by the 4A origin rule, snapshots, `segments` rows and per-branch `meta` deleted, generation bumped from rev 0, a `Rewound` event per branch; interim rule (d) gone, rule (b) counts the cascaded rows); `derived::recompute` after every rewind, diverged tail and cascade (stale `record_links` rows dropped, index rebuilt); cascade entry reusable for a voided `branch` record (`cascade::subtree` + `Cause::voided`)
- [x] Task 11: branch-aware verify, rebuild and compaction: verify resolves each 4B branch from the segments naming its index record at its fork point (`Known::for_branch`), `chain_continuity` starts at `forkSeq + 1` with `forkHash` as first parent, new check 9 `branch_index` (index and `record_links` against `derived::compute`, index rows without state, branches without index row, other segments of a branch directory named by a set-aside or unknown record only); rebuild gives the follower's DB back (branches, renames, archived, branch of branch, collisions); compaction resolves at the fork point, never packs across branch records and abandons a manifest naming another record's segment. Folded in the 9b re-review fix: a `branch` record that holds its id again after a recompute gets its fork state back in the same pass, `ID_COLLISION` follows the pass's verdicts, a reborn id forgets the old branch's rewinds
- [x] Task 12: undo and redo derived from the log: `fin_core::history` (`LogView`, per-author stacks per branch from its own records, `merged_away` drops for merges into non-descendants only, a redo record is its own unit, `plan_undo`/`plan_redo` rebase stored inverses over the records since with mirrors, full policy); `Store::undo`, `Store::redo` (through `Store::write`, links in `CommittedTx.link`, dry runs on followers, retries by txId), `Store::undo_stacks`; `HistoryResult`, `Stacks` in the generated types; `NOTHING_TO_UNDO`, `NOTHING_TO_REDO` (exit 3)
- [x] Task 13: merge through the rebase engine: `fin_core::history::{candidates, merge}` (common point of two lineages, candidates per Decision 19 with `UNDOES_TARGET_RECORDS` exclusions, steps = inverted source path + target path + planned copies with copy/original mirrors, copies keep the source's ids through `BoundIds`, `ALREADY_PRESENT` vs `ID_COLLISION` by origin-chain root, `DEPENDS_ON_SKIPPED`, full conflict list); `steps_from` mirrors an undo of a merge group; `Store::merge` (one transaction, `<mergeId>-<n>`, dedupe on `<mergeId>-1`, `argsHash`, mergeId/txId collisions refused, dry runs on followers), `MergeReport` in the generated types; `segment::set_max_records_for_test` (thread-local). Task 12 review folded in: redo stack dropped with the undo stack on merge, `HistoryReq.args_hash`. Proptest budget, second measure: `rebase_props` + `shift_map` + `undo` + `merge` 0.15 s wall (debug, Linux VM; budget < 15 s)
  - Review follow-up: transitive merges with presence by origin root (Decision 34: `origin::Roots`, same-root mirrors, `UNDOES_TARGET_RECORDS` through roots, dependents of excluded records `DEPENDS_ON_SKIPPED`, `ALREADY_PRESENT` per first binder's root), mergeId/txId guard on every write, `merged` state rechecked on a merge's target
- [x] Task 14: cherry-pick, revert and derived `merged` state. `fin_core::history::{pick, merged, origin}`: a cherry-pick re-runs one record through the merge's steps and names its origin root (with `originHash`), and is a no-op when the target already holds that root. A revert inverts a merge group newest first, rebased over later records, and is a no-op once the group's effect is gone. `steps_from` mirrors a revert with its group. `merged` (Decision 21, for merges into a non-descendant) is computed in `derived::compute` and kept incrementally on writes and import passes; a new edit reactivates the branch. `Store::cherry_pick`/`revert` (`argsHash`, retries from the log, dry runs on followers, a txId that is a mergeId refused). Followers derive the same states in every arrival order, and so does a rebuild
  - Review fix: mirror chains pair part by part and merges/picks pair with the copy in effect (merge after revert-then-undo, or undo-then-redo, keeps the source's order; property `pick_props`); the merged state follows every branch on a copy's origin chain; a copy of a revert counts as the revert; a revert lists every conflicting record; no-op picks/reverts answer `noOp` (`ALREADY_PRESENT`, `ALREADY_REVERTED`); on import, every branch given records or made by a `branch` record is rechecked (copies may arrive first; all 120 orders converge)

### Follow-ups from 4B
- [ ] In-band fork resolution: a record kind chain resolution honours, so `CHAIN_FORK` needs no manual move-out (Decision 29; today: the manual procedure plus `fin project recover --import`)
- [ ] `project.recover` changed its result from `{manual}` to the `RecoverReport` within protocol 1 (not additive; `manual` is still there): record it in protocol 2
- [ ] Conflict reasons: an undo whose clip is back already (re-created by a copy) is `ID_COLLISION`, where `ALREADY_PRESENT` would say it better (the undo planner does not judge origins)
- [ ] Conflict reasons: a copy whose clip is gone may answer the re-run's `NOT_FOUND` with no blamed record when an excluded inverted source undo lies between a mirrored pair; name the excluded record (`REMOVED`/`DEPENDS_ON_SKIPPED`)
- [ ] There is no `fin log` (or other) command to list txIds and mergeIds; `fin cherry-pick`'s `NOT_FOUND` fix names `fin log`, which does not exist (`fin-core/src/history/pick.rs`). Today ids come from each write's answer and `ops.subscribe`
- [ ] Perf: `undo --to` still reads the whole branch; merge planning is O(candidates × (source + target path)) even with prepared steps (800 + 800: 0.75 s debug); `derived::compute` (verify, full recompute) parses every record (222 ms at 1,500 + 1,500 with 1,500 copies); the recover listing (`Forest::load`) parses every current quarantine row; status counts quarantine through a covering index (7.6 ms at 10,000 rows)
- [ ] `recoverDismissed` is local to a server and lost on a rebuild (dismissed groups come back)
- [ ] `REBUILD_WOULD_LOSE.exportedThrough` reports only `main`'s
- [ ] History commands need a running server, also for `--dry-run` (no offline planning)
- [ ] `crates/fin/src/config.rs` module doc still shows the old flat `tries: {<branchId>: <parentId>}`; the file is per project (`tries: {<projectId>: {…}}`)
- [ ] Squashing archived branches (deferred out of sub-project 4; needs a two-phase delete)
- [ ] `BranchCreated`, `HistoryResult`, `MergeReport`, `RecoverReport` are `Schema.Unknown` in contracts (narrowed to generated types); generate Effect schemas with `ProjectStatus`

Then the `rebase_moves_props` oracle allows the unblamed partner-drift refusal (`keyed4c::partner_drift`, fixed case `partner_of_overwrite_move_refuses_unblamed`): a known limitation, see the follow-ups
- [x] Self relocation's refusal (2026-11-08; spec §4.3, §4.6): a workspace run of `rebase_moves_props` found a stale refusal `INSERT_INSIDE_CLIP` (unblamed) where the fresh edit applies. t1 holds c1, c2, c3; the head ripples c2 to the end, so c3 starts at frame 10; ours slides c3 by −1 and then ripple-moves c4 before c2 (frame 10). Frame 10 maps to 10 (c2's removed range collapses to its start), our rebased slide puts c3's start at 9 without a shift, so the move lands inside c3, which the head only shifted (no change to blame). This is the documented *self relocation* limitation (positions map through shifts only, and a slide records none), not an engine bug; the oracle allowed it in equality (`self_relocates`) but not in refusals. Now `keyed4c::self_drift` (the positional ops after our own slide or overwrite move) allows only the position-inside-an-item codes (`INSERT_INSIDE_CLIP`, `MOVE_INTO_ITSELF`, `OVERWRITE_SPLITS_ITEM`) unblamed; `self_relocates` is defined from it. Seed in `rebase_moves_props.proptest-regressions`, fixed case `slide_then_move_at_moved_anchor_refuses_unblamed`. Sibling search: all fin-core property binaries pass; the rebase ones (`rebase_moves_props`, `props_rebase_4c`, `props_places`, `caption_rebase`, `rebase_props`, `rebase`) at `PROPTEST_CASES=2000`, the first four also at 30,000 (1 min 55 s, debug)

    - `fin`: Linux 14.2 MB (11.4 MB stripped), macOS 11.7 MB, Windows 12.5 MB. `cargo tree -p fin` has no `fin-text`, `fin-fonts`, `fin-probe` or TLS crates.

      - `record` p99: macOS 375 ns diagnostics / 542 ns training, Windows 400 / 300 ns; budget ≤ 5 µs;

      - 482 B per diagnostics entry and 792 B with a 2-op intent (Windows read 629 B until `307ab74`).

    - `1de5910` fix(fin-node): a journal is keyed by the nearest existing ancestor made canonical. `Journal.open` keyed a not-yet-created server data by its raw path, while engines looked it up canonically, so on macOS (`/var` → `/private/var`) and Windows (`\\?\`) engines got a disabled journal and recorded nothing. finisherd creates server data first, so the shipped server was not affected. Test: `engines find a journal opened before its server data exists, behind a symlink` (red on Linux before the fix).
    - `76b81b3` test(fin): the `cli_diagnostics` fake listens on a named pipe on Windows.
    - `fbe6066` test(finisherd): the `locationOf` test builds native paths.
    - `307ab74` fix(journal): `journal_cost` stats files by path (a Windows directory entry's size lags for an open file).

    - `pnpm e2e` runs `<repo>/target/release/fin` whatever `CARGO_TARGET_DIR` says. Linked on Linux; run without it on macOS.

### Follow-ups from 4C
- [ ] `scripts/e2e/lib.mjs` ignores `CARGO_TARGET_DIR` (it runs `<repo>/target/release/fin`, possibly a stale build); found in Task 29
- [ ] `packages/fin-node/package.json` has no `files` list: a pack would carry `test/` and the test-hooks binary (24 MB); fix before the package ships
- [ ] `cargo clippy -p fin-journal` without `test-hooks` fails `default_constructed_unit_structs` (`Faults::default()`, `lib.rs:159`); CI runs no clippy
- [ ] macOS timing flakes seen once in Task 29: `storage.test.ts` "ENGINE_BUSY is passed through" (an auto backup `busy` right after the verify) and `engine.test.ts` 500-commit event-loop gap 26.9 ms > 20 ms
- [ ] **Partner drift** (spec §4.3, §5.11; merge `c42e9ae`): a stale positional op after our own overwrite move of a linked item can be refused by policy (`MOVE_INTO_ITSELF`), unblamed, where a fresh one succeeds, because an overwrite move records no shift and later ops anchored on a partner do not map with it. Fix: overwrite moves carry their partners' positions (a per-partner step), then drop the oracle's `keyed4c::partner_drift` allowance
- [ ] Drop the `clip.*` client aliases at schema 3 (spec §3.2)

- [ ] CI's wasm32-wasip1 steps (eval corpus, fin-text `measure` and `corpus_real`) have not completed a run on GitHub

## Sub-project 5A: principals, local credentials and scopes (in progress)

Spec: `docs/specs/2026-11-07-sub-project-5-control-plane-design.md` (revision 3). Plan: `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-11-07-sub-project-5a-principals-and-scopes.md`. Branch `sp5a-principals`, stacked on `sp4c-edit-breadth`. Tasks 1, 2 and 10 needed only D1, D2 and 4C; diagnostics D1–D7 are merged into this branch (2026-11-07), so Tasks 3–9 and 11–14 can proceed. 5C-1 builds on Tasks 1 and 2.

- [x] Task 1: control store (`control.db`, per-area migration chains), environment id, `server_ids`, audit log
- [x] Task 2: key files in `auth/`, the socket in `run/`, private server data on Windows, handshake crypto
- [x] Task 3: connection-aware protocol, principals, the handshake RPCs (not yet enforced). As built: finisherd serves `FinRpcs` through its own NDJSON protocol (`protocol.ts`) instead of Effect's `makeProtocolSocketServer`: it caps inbound lines at 16 MiB (`FRAME_TOO_LARGE` to the request id read from the line's start, then close; the WebSocket's `maxPayload` is the cap plus 64 KiB), answers bad JSON, unknown frames, unknown tags, bad headers and payloads its schema refuses with `INVALID_REQUEST` (decoded here with the codec and parse options `RpcServer` uses, so the server's own decode never fails; a frame with no id to answer closes the connection), drops `x-fin-` headers and registers each connection with a `Link` (close, close after the reply, answer open requests then close). `connections.ts` records Effect 4.0.6's facts (client ids per protocol, so `Connections` is per transport; disconnects; where decoding fails); `ConnectionRegistry` sums the transports. `auth/handshake.ts` (`Handshake`: hello with `envId`, canonical endpoint, `serverNonce` and the proof only for a live credential with matching `env`/`endpoint`; one prove per hello; five failures close after the reply; `auth.fail` audited with `transport`), `auth/gate.ts` (`withAuthentication`: every RPC but `server.hello`/`auth.prove`; refusals counted in `rpc.stats`), `principal.ts`, contracts `auth.ts` (`PrincipalSchema`, `CredentialRefSchema`, the hello and `auth.prove` schemas, `PUBLIC_RPCS`, `defaultScopes`) and `scopes.ts` (`SCOPES`, `ScopeSchema`, `HUMAN_ONLY`; Task 5 adds the rules); `RPC_METHODS` += `auth.prove`; audit details gain `transport`. The WebSocket's connections start as `local` (its bearer token is a file of the user's, like `local.key`) until Task 6
- [x] Task 4: `fin` proves; the server requires proofs; exit code 7. As built: `crates/fin/src/auth.rs` (`discover`: `FIN_CREDENTIAL` → a `{v:1, kind: agent|mcp, id, key, env}` file, else `<server-data>/auth/local.key`; keys decode strictly (43 canonical base64url chars); an unreadable or malformed credential is none; `handshake`: hello `{clientNonce, env?, endpoint, credential}`, checks `envId`, the canonical endpoint and the proof (HMAC-SHA-256, constant time), then `auth.prove {mac}`; `connect` retries once after re-reading `finisherd.json` (unless `FIN_SERVER`) and the key, noting `keyStale` when the key changed; a second failure is `SERVER_UNTRUSTED` `{endpoint, reason: noProof|badProof|envMismatch|endpointMismatch}` with the fix in words; no credential → no handshake, and the server's `UNAUTHENTICATED` gains `reason: noCredential` and a fix naming `FIN_CREDENTIAL` and the server's data; test hook `FIN_TEST_AUTH_GATE`), `fin whoami` → `{principal, retried?}` (`anonymous` without a credential; `cli.command` name `whoami`), exit 7 `DENIED` for `DENIED_CODES` (11 codes); `INVALID_REQUEST` → 3, `FRAME_TOO_LARGE` → 2. `console` is not a fin credential kind (it is `finisherd <command>`'s). finisherd: `ENFORCE` and the legacy `local` principal are removed rather than flipped (an unproved connection is `anonymous`; `auth.legacy` stays a readable audit kind, never written); TS raw-socket tests prove first (`ndjson-client.ts`). `scripts/e2e/lib.mjs` needed no change: e2e talks over the WebSocket, `local` until Task 6
- [x] Task 5: middleware chain, scopes, reach, policy and the write guard. As built: contracts `scopes.ts` gains `FinRpcTag`, `RPC_SCOPES` (every RPC: its scopes, all required; resolvers; escalations; `writes`, the payload fields naming a write target) and `requiredScopes`; `chain/` in finisherd (`chain.ts` `withChain` replaces `auth/gate.ts`, unary and stream alike; `resolve.ts`, `authorize.ts`, `policy.ts`, `fixes.ts`): authenticate → resolve (projects, `openDir` with `finisher.project.json`'s id and whether it is open here, `createDir`, `probePath` and `importDir` canonical; the handler gets the canonical probe path) → authorise (human-only first, then every scope, then reach: `principal.projects` → `PROJECT_NOT_FOUND`, paths outside the principal's open projects' folders → `PATH_NOT_ALLOWED`) → policy (branch write targets resolved through the store, then agent branch limits: `"own"` = branches whose `createdBy` is `<kind>:<id>`; `ask` → `APPROVAL_REQUIRED {approvalId}`, approvals in memory) → handler with `options.guard`/`options.principal` → after (`project.open` must open the project its folder named for principals with project limits). Branch references resolve after authorisation, not before: resolving needs the project's engine, which an unauthorised caller must not get opened, and only the policy reads them. Every denial is audited (`auth.denied {rpc, rule, scope|scopes, reason, transport}`; `UNAUTHENTICATED` denials aggregate per minute like `auth.fail`) and counted in `rpc.stats`. The guard rides in each write's `metaJson` (`{actor, guard}`) to fin-node, which applies it around every store write (`Store::with_guard`); fin-store checks it in `WriteCtx::target`, inside the IMMEDIATE transaction, after resolving the target branch (`BRANCH_NOT_ALLOWED`, `BRANCH_PROTECTED`). `project.takeover {confirm: true}` and `project.open {relocate: true}` are human-only now: their tests use the console (`crates/fin/tests/support/console.rs`; the `project-open-relocate` fixture is captured and replayed as `console`). There are no job or agent RPCs in `FinRpcs` yet (5C-2 and 7A-2 add them): the mapped type makes each new one a type error until it has a rule
- ~~Task 7 (from Task 5)~~ (done in Task 7): the diagnostics RPCs keep `diagnostics.read` in `RPC_SCOPES` until Task 7 makes `diagnostics.consent`, `diagnostics.purge`, `diagnostics.project {collect: "default"}` and content exports need `diagnostics.admin` (an `escalate` on each), with `human_only_matrix` (`apps/finisherd/test/chain.test.ts`) extended to them and their fin and e2e steps moved to the console in the same commit. No `agent`/`mcp` credential exists before Task 9, so nothing is exposed meanwhile
- Task 9 (from Task 5): agent credentials' authors: the chain's `"own"` compares a branch's `createdBy` with `<kind>:<id>` (`chain/policy.ts` `authorOf`); since the Task 5 review the server stamps it on `branch.create` and `project.recover` for branch-limited principals (`RuleOf.author`); other records' `author` fields still come from the payload
- Review of Task 5 (fixed on `sp5a-t6`, commit `fix(auth): Task 5 review`): H1 every write of a branch-limited principal carries `allowBranches` (rules that write declare `writes`, `[]` allowed; `WRITE_RPCS`), and a write without the chain's guard, or limited without an allow-list, is a defect (`writeGuardOf`, also L3); H2 `project.open`/`create` get the canonical folder, `registry.open` checks the project the chain read before registering it (`PROJECT_NOT_FOUND {reason: changed}`) and only attaches when loading was not checked; M1 serving a project that is not open needs `project.write` (`RuleFacts.reopens`); M2 paths canonicalise through their nearest existing ancestor, and `asset.probe` re-checks the real path around the probe for principals with roots; M3 `finisher.project.json` is opened `O_NOFOLLOW|O_NONBLOCK`, a regular file of at most 64 KiB; M4 authors stamped (above); M5 `auth.denied` aggregates per `(principal, code, rpc)` per minute and stream refusals are counted in `rpc.stats`; L1 human-only, then the RPC's own scopes, then reach, then the facts' escalations (no open-state oracle); L2 `allowBranches: null` refused, the generated type has no `| null`; L5 escalations typed by their RPC's payload (`RuleOf<T>`); L7 `fin` reads only regular key files, opened non-blocking
- **Before Task 9** (the first `agent`/`mcp` credentials), from the Task 5 review: ~~Task 7 is a hard prerequisite~~ (done); **L4** streams are authorised once, at subscribe: revocation and expiry must end live streams (`Connections.closeWhere` and the stream's own fiber), not only refuse new calls; residuals Task 9 must close or accept: the probe's real path is re-checked around the probe, not on the opened file (an fd-based check in fin-node), and a project evicted between the chain's open-state check and the handler is reopened for a `project.read` caller
- Sub-project 7 (from the Task 5 review, **L6**): cap `approvals` (the chain's in-memory `ask` map) and expire its entries when approvals become real
- [x] Task 6: WebSocket tickets, Host and Origin checks, protocol 2. As built: `PROTOCOL_VERSION = 2` (contracts and `fin`); `tx.commit.generation` and `ops.subscribe.generation` required; `asset.probe {projectId, path}` (resolvers `project` + `probePath`: reach on the project, and for principals with roots the path must be in that project's folder; the handler opens the project on demand, `PROJECT_NOT_FOUND` otherwise; `fin` sends the project it opened); `auth.ticket {ttlS?}` (`server.read`; 30 s default, capped at 60, at least 1; 32 random bytes base64url; only `SHA-256(ticket)` kept with the principal and expiry; one use, an expired ticket is used up by the attempt too; swept every 10 s; at most 1,024 live, the oldest dropped; `auth.ticket` audited), `auth.whoami` and `auth.revokeSelf` (no id: an `agent`/`mcp` principal revokes its own credential through `HandshakeDeps.revoke`, which Task 9 provides, its tickets are dropped and the connection closes after the reply; `local`/`console` get `INVALID_REQUEST`). `upgrade.ts`: our own `node:http` server on `127.0.0.1:0` with `ws` (now a direct, pinned dependency, 8.22.0) in `noServer` mode; checks path `/rpc` (404), `finisher.v2` offered (426 `{"protocol":{"min":2,"max":2}}`), Host in `127.0.0.1|localhost|[::1]:<port>` (403), Origin absent, one of those `http://` origins or in `settings.json` `server.origins` (403), then exactly one `finisher.ticket.<t>` that is live (401); answers `Sec-WebSocket-Protocol: finisher.v2`; `?ticket=` is never read; refusals other than path and version are audited `auth.fail {credentialKind: ticket, reason}` (aggregated). Each accepted socket carries its ticket's principal into `protocol.ts` (`UpgradePrincipal`, `Connections.attach(…, principal)`): the WebSocket no longer starts as `local`. `finisherd.json` has no `token`. `scripts/e2e/lib.mjs` `connectWs(server)` proves over the socket (`socketCall`, the same MACs, checked against the shared vectors in `scripts/e2e.test.mjs`) and mints the ticket. The RPC fixtures are re-captured: writes carry a generation, the incarnation is normalised to `01fixtureincarnation000000` and `rpc_contract.rs` substitutes this run's; request ids restart at 1 after the handshake. `fin`'s `versionMismatch` is now `protocolVersion < 2` alone
- ~~Task 7 (from Task 6)~~ (done in Task 7): the WebSocket's journal source is still D4's per-transport `ui`, so a `local` ticket over the WebSocket can still opt in to training (`diagnostics.consent training on`) until Task 7 takes sources from principals (`sourceOf`) and makes training on device-only. `scripts/e2e/lib.mjs` `socketCall` and `apps/finisherd/test/support/ws-client.ts` are the helpers to extend with the console challenge
- [x] Task 7: diagnostics sources and actors from principals; human-only diagnostics. As built: `fin_core::events::Source` gains `console` and `mcp` (`mcp` emits `cli.command` and `error.panic`, `console` nothing); `RPC_METHODS` += `console.challenge`, `CLI_FLAGS` += `content`. finisherd: D4's per-transport `RpcSource` is gone; `sourceOf(principal)` (`local`/`agent` → `fin`, `mcp` → `mcp`, `console` → `console`, `device` → `ui`) and `actorFor(principal, headers)` (`agent`/`mcp` always `{agent, session: <credential id>}`, only `call` from the header; `device` `{user, session}`; `console` `{user}`; `local` D6's header actor); one run id per connection across transports; the `diagnostics.record` rate limit per principal (`local`, `console`, `<kind>:<id>`, at most 1,024 buckets). `RPC_SCOPES`: `diagnostics.consent` and `diagnostics.purge` need `diagnostics.admin`; `diagnostics.project {collect: default}` and `diagnostics.export {content: true}` escalate to it; rules gain `deviceOnly` (training on → `CONSENT_UI_ONLY` for every principal but a `device`, before the human check, the console included; its fix in words, `where: app`) and `confirm` (`project.open`, `project.takeover`, `diagnostics.consent|purge|project`). The server half of Decision 30: `console.challenge {}` (`auth.admin`, console principals only; `INVALID_REQUEST` otherwise) → a 6-character Crockford code valid 60 s, kept (as SHA-256) on the connection itself, at most 4 live; the chain's last stage refuses a `confirm` call that needs a human-only scope from a `console` principal without its connection's live code (`CONFIRMATION_REQUIRED`, exit 7, audited `console.refused`); typed codes are read the Crockford way (case, I/L/O); a code is used by the call it confirms, a wrong answer voids all of the connection's codes. The payloads of the confirmed RPCs take `challenge`. Exports by a principal that is not a person are redacted (`ExportPage.redact()` in fin-journal: content fields and their blobs removed, `redacted: true`; fin-node `export(…, redact)`). Consent changes and purges are audited (`consent.change {purpose, value}`, `diagnostics.purge {reason: all|project|before}`). `fin diagnostics export --content` sends `content: true` (`HUMAN_ONLY` for `fin`). Test helpers: `confirmed()` (`ndjson-client.ts`), `WsClient.confirmed`, `Console::confirmed`/`confirmed_ok` (`crates/fin/tests/support/console.rs`), `socketCall(…, {confirm})`, `consoleCall` and `storedEntries` (`scripts/e2e/lib.mjs`). The RPC fixtures of the confirmed calls are captured as the console with the code normalised to `0FIXTURECHALLENGE0`, which `rpc_contract.rs` replaces with its own connection's; `diagnostics-consent-human-only` records `fin`'s refusal. README's diagnostics section says which commands are the owner's
- Task 8 (from Task 7): the server half of the confirmations exists; the console CLI calls `console.challenge` on its own connection, shows the code, and sends the RPC with `challenge: <typed>` only after the person types it (a refusal says `reason: missing|wrong|expired`; after `wrong` ask for a new code). `finisherd diagnostics consent training on` already answers `CONSENT_UI_ONLY` from the server; the console may also refuse it locally. Content exports need no code (not a change)
- Review of Task 6 and its Task 5 fixes (fixed on `sp5a-t7`, commit `fix(auth): Task 6 review`): **1** fin-probe opens every probed or fingerprinted file without blocking (`open.rs`: `O_NONBLOCK`, then fstat, anything but a regular file is `ASSET_UNREADABLE {reason: NotAFile}`, then `O_NONBLOCK` cleared), so a FIFO cannot hold a libuv thread; **2** principals with roots probe through `probeBeneath(root, path)` (fin-node; `fin_probe::probe_beneath`): Linux `openat2(RESOLVE_BENEATH|RESOLVE_NO_SYMLINKS)` with a per-component `openat(O_NOFOLLOW)` walk as fallback (also macOS and other Unix), Windows `FILE_FLAG_OPEN_REPARSE_POINT` plus `GetFinalPathNameByHandleW` under the root; a dangling or swapped symlink is `PATH_NOT_ALLOWED` and nothing of its target is read (`stillCanonical` is gone); **3** one revoke path (`auth/revoke.ts` `revokeCredential`: the store, the credential's tickets, and every live connection it proved on every transport, ended with `CREDENTIAL_REVOKED`, which ends its streams; `auth.revokeSelf` uses it and closes its own connection after the reply; Task 9's owner revoke and expiry sweep must call it); **4** tickets: at most 8 live per principal (a newer one drops that principal's oldest), 4 mints/s per principal (burst 16), and past the global 1,024 a mint is refused (`TICKET_LIMIT`, exit 5) instead of evicting another principal's ticket; **5** `project.open` by a principal with project limits answers a folder of another project exactly like a folder with none (`PROJECT_NOT_FOUND`, no id); **6** `Engine.open(…, {expectId})` (`OpenOptions::expect_id`): the store refuses a folder that no longer holds the checked project before it rebuilds, locks or leases anything; **7** rules gain `targets` (branches acted on without a guarded write): `project.acknowledgeRewind` is branch-limited; **8** the WebSocket listener cuts off clients that take over 10 s for their headers (checked every second), holds at most 64 connections, and re-reads `settings.json`'s origins only when the file changes (`cachedOrigins`)
- **9** (accepted residual, review of Task 6): a project evicted between the chain's open-state check and its handler is reopened for a `project.read` caller (the chain saw it open, so no `project.write` was asked for). Reopening takes the lease as any open does; accepted because eviction follows a dead engine, and the caller could have read it a moment earlier
- **10** (for Task 9, review of Task 6): `rootsOf` knows only the open projects of a principal with roots, so `asset.probe` for one of its projects that is not open is `PATH_NOT_ALLOWED` even though the handler would open it. Task 9 should take the folder from the registry index (`app.db`) for the principal's own projects, or open the project first
- Review of Task 7 and the Task 6 fixes (fixed on `sp5a-t8`, commit `fix(auth): Task 7 review`): **M1** `local` (every `fin` and tool of the user) has its ticket mint bucket and 8-ticket cap per minting connection (`local:<transport>:<clientId>`), under `MAX_TICKETS_LOCAL` (64) live and `LOCAL_MINT_RATE` (16/s, burst 64) for `local` as a whole, refused rather than evicting; the `diagnostics.record` limiter is per connection for `local` too, under `LOCAL_RECORD_RATE` (200/s, burst 800); **M2** the WebSocket listener caps sockets that have not upgraded on their own (`MAX_UNAUTHENTICATED` 16; one that does not upgrade within the header timeout is closed, also when it sends nothing), and WebSockets per ticket owner (8), for `local` as a whole (32) and in all (64, of which 16 only for `device` and `console`): past a cap the upgrade is 503 `tooMany` (not audited as an auth failure); **M3** `ConnectionRegistry` keeps the credentials revoked while the server runs: a connection registering with one (a ticket taken before the revocation) is closed at once, and every request re-checks (`principalOf` is `anonymous`, the chain answers `CREDENTIAL_REVOKED`, audited `auth.denied {rule: revoked}`); **L2** a call that needs a human-only scope needs the console's typed code by default (`needsConfirmation`); a rule opts out only with `unconfirmed: readOnly | service` (`console.challenge` and `diagnostics.export` today); **L3** `probeBeneath` opens its root `O_NOFOLLOW` (Windows: no reparse point), and `probe.ts` asks `rootsOf` whether a principal is restricted, as the chain does; **L4** exports carry content only with `content: true` (a person's export without it is redacted too); **L5** a redacted export page drops entries of kinds or kind versions this build does not know, as the upload view does. Without a credential store (5A) a revocation is audited (`auth.revokeSelf` leaves a `credential.revoke` row); with Task 9's store, only a credential that was live
- **Task 9 prerequisite** (review of Task 7, M3): a write already handed to fin-node's worker when its credential is revoked still commits. Carry a credential epoch (or the credential id) in the `WriteGuard` and have fin-store re-check liveness inside its write transaction, so that a revoked credential's write in flight is refused; until then the window is one in-flight write per connection
- Task 13 README (review of Task 7, L1): the console's typed challenge keeps `yes |` pipes and pasted scripts out; it does not stop a same-user process that can read `<server-data>/auth/` (`console.key`) or drive a pseudo-terminal (owner decision 6; sub-project 7 closes it)
- Accepted residual (review of Task 7, L3): `probeBeneath` sets `RESOLVE_BENEATH | RESOLVE_NO_SYMLINKS` but not `RESOLVE_NO_XDEV`, so a mount point inside a project folder is followed; and a hard link inside the folder to a file outside it is a regular file there and is probed (a path rule cannot tell it apart). Both need the user's own write access inside the project folder

A program that drives a pseudo-terminal (`script`, `expect`) passes the terminal check: the typed code keeps pipes and pasted scripts out, not same-user code (owner decision 6; README in Task 13). New RPCs (`server-admin.ts`): `server.status` (`server.read`: version, protocol, envId, pid, listeners, connections per kind, journal, audit failures, warnings), `server.stop` (`auth.admin`, `unconfirmed: service`: answers `{stopping: true}`, and once the reply is written (`Link.afterReply`) `serve` returns and its finalizers stop everything; exit 0), `auth.audit.list` (`auth.admin`, read-only) and `auth.audit.purge` (`auth.admin`, confirmed; `{all: true} | {before}`; audited afterwards as the new kind `audit.purge {reason, count}`). The console-side TTY refusal is not audited (the server never hears of it); `console.refused` stays the server's missing or wrong code

- Task 12: the e2e `auth` scenario must exercise the socket proofs end to end (`fin` and the console over `fin.sock`, a squatted socket, a stale key), not only the WebSocket: e2e talks over the WebSocket today, so no e2e step proves over the socket yet.
- Windows: `fin` checks a credential file's owner and mode on Unix only; on Windows the private `auth/` directory (Task 2) protects `local.key`, and Task 9 should check a `FIN_CREDENTIAL` file's DACL the same way.

Notes for later tasks (review of Tasks 1, 2, 10):
- Task 3: finisherd caps its own inbound lines at 16 MiB (socket and WebSocket, `FRAME_TOO_LARGE`); today only `fin` caps what it reads.
- Sub-project 9 (packaging): the control store's checkpoint worker is started with `new Worker(new URL("./checkpoint-worker.ts", import.meta.url))` and relies on Node's type stripping; a bundled or packaged finisherd must ship that file (or a compiled `.js`, with the URL changed) next to `control/db.ts`.

- [x] Task 4: Unix host death (watchdog), cgroups, memory limits, foreign processes, abandonment. As built: `fin-workers watchdog <host pid>` (`watchdog.rs`, 195 lines; libc is a plain Unix dependency again): `+g`/`-g`/`+c`/`-c` lines; host exit seen with `pidfd_open` + `poll` (Linux) or `kqueue` `EVFILT_PROC`/`NOTE_EXIT` (macOS), plus a `getppid` check each second; then a non-blocking drain of stdin (what the dead host wrote is in the pipe, so a late `-g` counts), `kill(-pgid, SIGKILL)` and `cgroup.kill` ← `1`, exit 0; it ignores `SIGINT`/`SIGTERM`/`SIGHUP`/`SIGPIPE` and then writes `ready` on stdout, which the host waits for (a signal right after `exec` killed it 3 runs in 20 before the handshake); started in its own process group, empty environment, cwd `/`, stdin/stdout pipes and stderr only (Linux `close_range` in `pre_exec`, macOS `posix_spawn` with `CLOEXEC_DEFAULT`), never with PDEATHSIG; the host side (`unix/watchdog.rs`) keeps the list and writes each change under one lock; a monitor thread reaps it, logs, restarts (first at once, then at most once a minute) and re-sends the list; dropping it closes its stdin and waits ≤ 2 s for it to exit; `caps().watchdog`; process groups write `+g` at the group's first `placed` and `-g` at `close_group` (before the leader reap: `minus_g_precedes_the_leader_reap`). Cgroups (`linux/cgroup.rs`, 400 lines): `detect()` needs `0::<path>` on cgroup v2 (mount point from `/proc/self/mounts`), `cgroup.procs` = only us (or, for a second host in the process, `<cg>/host` with us and our children and an empty `<cg>`), `cgroup.kill`, write access to the dir, `cgroup.procs` and `cgroup.subtree_control`; else a logged reason and process groups; `FIN_CGROUPS=off` or `CgroupMode::Off` skip it; setup moves the host into `<cg>/host`, enables the available `+memory +pids` on `<cg>`, `<cg>/jobs` and each group; groups are `jobs/g<n>` (`<n>` unique per process) with `memory.max`, `memory.swap.max` 0 (else the limit is passed by swapping) and `memory.oom.group` 1; leaves `g<n>/p<k>`, the child writes `0` to the leaf's `cgroup.procs` in `pre_exec`; `pids.max` counts threads, so `active_processes: n` becomes `n × 256` tasks (a fork-storm stop, not an exact process count; deviation); OOM = `memory.events` `oom_kill > 0`, peak = `memory.peak`; the first start in a process sweeps leftover `jobs/g*` (kill, remove deepest first). Memory: `RssAnon + RssShmem` (Linux; a file on tmpfs, where this repo's test runner keeps `TMPDIR`, counts as shmem, correctly) and `ri_phys_footprint` (macOS); with process groups a sampler on the timer thread checks each limited group every `rss_sample_ms` and kills it over the limit, every member `Oom` (`for_others` keeps `Oom`), the highest sample as `memory_peak`; `MemoryRule {fixed_mib, ram_fraction, cap_mib}::resolve(total)` in Rust (exported for the engine through napi). Foreign processes: scanned at each `result`, before each kill and at each exit (`/proc/*/stat` pgrp, `proc_listpids(PROC_PGRP_ONLY)`, or the group's `cgroup.procs` tree) into a per-group seen set, so a grandchild killed with its group still counts. Abandonment: every kill (not only `kill_group`) starts a kill wait; a process the kill should have ended that is still alive after `kill_wait_ms` is reported once as `Abandoned`, released from the tables (`GroupEmpty`, callbacks) and logged when it finally exits; test hook `waiter_delay_for_test`. Test hooks `Host::{watchdog_pid_for_test, watchdog_line_for_test, watchdog_groups_for_test}`. `test.oom` gained `mapFile` and `readyFile`; `test.echo`'s `cgroup` report is read by the first statement of `main`. Tests: `host_death_unix.rs` (12; the host is this test binary re-executed and `SIGKILL`ed), `host_cgroup.rs` (10; each test re-runs itself in a `systemd-run --user -p Delegate=yes --wait --pipe` unit and is skipped with a notice without one), `host_memory.rs` (4 on Linux). The kill tests (death, cgroup, memory and `host_unix`'s cancel and close tests) passed 20 runs in a row on Linux. Documented gap (Decision 18): with process groups a `setsid` grandchild survives cancel and host death (`setsid_grandchild_escape_is_documented` asserts it survives); cgroup mode kills it (`cgroup_kill_takes_setsid_grandchild`, `setsid_grandchild_gone_after_host_sigkill`). Not run here: macOS (compiled and linted for `aarch64-apple-darwin`)

  - Merged with Task 4 (`sp5c1-hosts`): one `close_leaf(&GroupHandle, LeafHandle)` with Task 4's timing (the group holds its leaves and releases them before `close_group`, or at once when a spawn fails; a Linux leaf cgroup cannot be removed at its process's exit without killing what the process left in it), so a Windows leaf Job stays open until its group is released and the per-leaf foreign count reads it live until then; `close_group(&GroupHandle)` always runs (the Job domain unregisters the port key; the group Job closes when the last handle clone drops); `hello` kept; Task 4's `run_done` (`catch_unwind`) and failed-spawn settle (`GroupEmpty`, callbacks, release) are shared code, so they cover the Windows spawn path; the pgid store-after-`placed` rule is Unix only; the waiter-start fallback works on Windows too (the process handle is kept in a slot when the waiter thread does not start, then `os::kill_one` (`TerminateProcess`) and the wait run on the spawn thread); the Job domain's `close_group` terminates the group Job, since a handle held elsewhere no longer closes it at once; `Host::send` names the running job **before** the `start` is queued and restores the previous one if it was not (Task 4's "only when queued" order let a fast worker's `result` arrive first and read as `otherJob`: `result_then_immediate_exit_is_success` failed 5 runs in 5 under 12 busy loops, 0 in 5 after); `src/tests.rs`'s failed-spawn and panicking-callback tests run on both (the stale-pgid one is Unix only)
  - Review of the merge (`sp5c1-hosts-fix`, `fix(fin-jobhost): harden the Windows spawn broker and close review gaps`): the spawn broker is the bundled `fin-workers.exe --spawn-broker` (exits 0 at once if resumed), else `%SystemRoot%\System32\cmd.exe /d /c exit 0` (always present, WRP-protected, found with `GetSystemDirectoryW`; `/d` skips AutoRun; `conhost.exe` rejected: it opens a console window if resumed), never `current_exe()`; made at `Host::start` with a protected DACL `D:P(A;;GA;;;SY)(A;;0x0;;;OW)` on the process and its thread; no broker → `HostError::Unavailable {reason: noBroker}` (`WORKER_UNAVAILABLE`), `inherit.rs` and the flip fallback removed; an outer Job's active-process limit → `{reason: processLimit}` (`ERROR_NOT_ENOUGH_QUOTA`; measured: the host and the broker take 2 slots, each worker 1, console hosts none; a Job with 1 slot gives `noBroker`); the outer Job's memory limit (null-handle query at start) makes an abnormal exit after a new outer peak above it `Oom` (without the check it read as `Exited` 0xC0000409); `SILENT_BREAKAWAY_OK` outer Jobs are harmless (the broker breaks away, processes are born outside it, all die with the host); process groups `kill(-pgid)` at `close_group` before `-g`; `Host::send` restores the previous job only while the slot still holds its own; test hooks `broker_images_for_test`, `after_send_for_test`, `fail_waiter_thread_for_test`, `Host::broker_for_test`. **Broker cost** (Windows 11, release, suspended): `fin-workers.exe` 0.4 MB commit / 2.0 MB working set / 1.2–1.4 ms (31 ms cold); `cmd.exe` 1.5 MB / 2.3 MB / ≈ 1 ms (5 ms cold); for comparison an Electron app's own exe 7.7–8.8 MB / 4.2–4.7 MB / 24–77 ms cold, `node.exe` 1.2 MB / 2.0 MB. The DACL test impersonates the user without `SeDebugPrivilege` (the OpenSSH session has it enabled, which opens any process). New tests fail without their fix: `close_group_ends_what_a_member_left` (grandchild outlives the release), `an_answer_before_send_returns_is_on_its_job` (`otherJob` 3/3), `outer_memory_limit_is_oom_not_a_crash`. Verified: Linux fin-jobhost 15 unit + fin-workers all suites green, kill suites ×10 (9/10; the failure is the pre-existing cgroup EBUSY flake below), workspace clippy (with and without test hooks, and for `x86_64-pc-windows-gnu`), fmt, `pnpm gates`; Windows 11 fin-jobhost 19 unit + all suites (broker 7, common 27, death 4, windows 18), kill and death suites ×20 (75 tests a run: 19/20; the failure is the pre-existing WER flake below), clippy with and without test hooks. - [x] Task 6: napi `JobHost`, params validation, codes. As built: `crates/fin-node/src/jobhost/{mod, events, napi_types, params}.rs` (mod 320 lines); values cross as JSON strings (the binding's convention), parsed on the JS thread by `packages/fin-node/jobhost.js` (new: `index.js` was at 585 lines, so the loader and error adapter moved to `native.js` and `index.js` re-exports); typings in `index.d.ts` (`JobHostOptions`, `JobHostEvent`, `JobHostExitInfo`, `JobSpawnRequest`, `JobHostCaps`; `Hello`, `HostMsg`, `WorkerMsg` from `@finisher/core-types`). **Deviations:** `JobHost.start` returns `Promise<JobHost>` (`Host::start` waits for the watchdog's `ready` on Unix and makes the spawn broker on Windows, so it runs on a `jobhost-start` thread: Decision 11a); the event queue is bounded by our own in-flight count (a reader waits on a condvar, then a non-blocking call into an unref'd, unbounded threadsafe function) instead of napi's blocking mode, which blocks inside napi-rs's abort read lock that the JS thread takes at teardown; `send` is `false` for a full queue **and** a gone process, and throws `INVALID_REQUEST` for a message that is not a `HostMsg` or too long; `abandonForTest(proc, holdMs = 60000)`; `spawn_and_kill_are_async` bounds every synchronous call and every event callback at 5 ms rather than a timer-lag monitor (a timer's lag on a loaded VM measures the machine). Events: per process `hello`, messages, `stderr`, `exit`; `groupEmpty` after the group's last exit; progress coalesced in `events.rs` (the newest kept, at most every 250 ms, a trailing one handed over by the `jobhost-events` flusher, any other event of the process flushes it first); `exit.info` is `{reason, kill? | what? | expected?/got?, code?, signal?, foreign?, memoryPeak?, stderrTail, lifetimeMs}`; **a process's events can reach JS before its `spawn` promise settles** (separate queues). The host is dropped on a `jobhost-drop` thread (its drop joins the spawn thread, which may be handing an event to the JS thread). Errors: `HostError` → its `code()` with `details.reason` (the named reason, else `unknownGroup`/`groupClosed`/`shutDown`/`program`/`spawner`/`spawn`) and `details.error`; unreadable arguments → `INVALID_REQUEST {field}`. Also: `validateJobParams` (static `PARAMS: &[&[KindParams]]`, `fin_jobs::test_kinds::PARAMS` under `test-hooks`; unknown kind or version → `{ok: false}`), `jobKindVersions`, `resolveMemoryRule(rule, totalBytes)` (Task 4's `MemoryRule::resolve`), `workersBinaryPath(dir?)` (pure `workersFileName`/`workersBinaryPath(dir, platform)` in `loader.js`). Test hooks: `fin-jobhost` gains `Host::kill_proc_for_test` (`SIGKILL` / `TerminateProcess` 137, reported `exited`) and `Host::abandon_for_test` (the waiter holds the observed exit, so the kill wait abandons it); `pauseEventsForTest` sleeps the handing-over threads. Codes (Decision 41) in `crates/fin/src/output.rs`: 3 `INVALID_REQUEST` (the same line and comment as 5A Task 3's, so the merge is clean), `JOB_KIND_UNKNOWN`, `JOB_PARAMS_INVALID`, `JOB_NOT_FOUND`; 4 a new `JOB_RESULT_CODES` list; 5 `JOB_QUEUE_FULL`, `WORKER_UNAVAILABLE`, `WORKER_PROTOCOL_MISMATCH`, `WAIT_TIMEOUT`, `RESOURCE_STUCK`, `WORKER_OOM`; `UNKNOWN_MESSAGE`/`CALL_LIMIT` stay internal (6); `JOBHOST_UNSUPPORTED` not added (Task 5 landed, nothing returns it). The contracts had no code list: `JOB_ERROR_CODES` (code → exit class) in `jobs.ts`, `jobs.test.ts` reads `output.rs`'s lists and checks it. `test_kinds_match_rust_params` no longer skips. Tests: `packages/fin-node/test/jobhost.test.ts` (15: the plan's 12 plus `test_hooks_kill_one_and_abandon`, `bad_arguments_are_invalid_request`, `resolve_memory_rule_matches_rust`), 3 runs in a row green on Linux; mutation `PROGRESS_EVERY` = 0 fails `progress_is_coalesced` and `slow_js_backpressures_the_worker`. **Review fixes:** the event queue is bounded by count and by summed JSON bytes (`eventQueueBytes`, 16 MiB; one larger event goes alone); `spawned`/`exit`/`groupEmpty` and spawn answers are not counted, so the spawn thread does not stall behind a slow JS thread (what remains is in Decision 11a); `fin-jobhost` sends `HostEvent::Spawned{proc, group}` and runs a spawn's callback before it and before the readers start, and `spawn` resolves through the event queue (`_spawn`, consumed by `jobhost.js`), so no event of a process precedes its `spawn` resolution; every promise is a `Settle` guard (`INTERNAL {reason: "unanswered"}` on drop; `failForTest: startThread | hostStart | spawn`, with `HostOptions::panic_on_{start,spawn}_for_test`); params over 512 KiB are `{ok: false}`, `version` is an `f64` checked as a `u32`; the JSON shapes are `fin_jobhost::wire` types generated into core-types (`JobHostOptions`, `JobGroupSpec`, `JobSpawnRequest`, `JobWatch`, `JobMemoryRule`, `JobHostKillReason`, `JobHostEvent`, `JobHostExitInfo`, `JobHostCaps`; `caps()` is JSON now); `send` answers `"ok" | "full" | "gone"`; `UnknownGroup`/`GroupClosed`/`ShutDown` are `JOBHOST_STATE` (3), `HostError::Unsupported` is gone; the contracts parity test checks both directions; `ProcState::reaped` stops `kill_proc_for_test` (now `-> bool`) signalling a reaped pid; `shutdown(graceMs)` rejects bad arguments; `test.progressFlood` takes `stderrBytes`. Tests: jobhost.test.ts 22 (5 runs in a row green); the `eventQueue: 8` and `eventQueueBytes` floods fail with the count or byte cap at `usize::MAX`; `kill_by_pid_skips_a_reaped_process` fails without the `reaped` check; timing bounds are an event-loop p99, hand-over stamps (`handedAtMsForTest`) and `FIN_STRICT_TIMING=1` for the absolute ones. Cross-checked: `fin-jobhost` clippy for `x86_64-pc-windows-gnu` and `aarch64-apple-darwin` with `test-hooks`; `fin-node` cannot be cross-linted here (`ring` needs a C cross-compiler). Not run: Windows, macOS

- [x] Task 9a: engine: submit, cold runs, files, host calls, hooks. As built: `apps/finisherd/src/jobs/{engine, runner, host, files, layer, view, calls}.ts` (engine 499 lines, runner 479). `JobEngine` as frozen; `JobEngineTag` is a `Context.Service` in `engine.ts`; **`layerJobEngine`, `buildJobEngine` (the engine as an Effect, the caller closes it) and `JobEngineOptions` live in `layer.ts`** (it imports `engine.ts`; `engine.ts` re-exporting them would be a cycle). `JobEngineOptions` adds `watchdog`, `diagnostics: () => boolean` (`start.diagnostics`, Decision 7; default off until D4), `queueLimit` (50,000) and `testKinds: {overrides, families}` (fields of test kinds and families replaced by name, for the engine's tests; `registerTestKinds` takes the same); `clock` defaults to the layer's `Clock`, and every Effect the engine runs (hooks, calls, logs) runs in the layer's context with that clock. `WorkersView` = `{pools, warm: [], processes, stuck, caps, diagDropped}`. Submit as Decision 27, plus: the decoded params are stored; a kind whose `files` is a function has them validated at submit (`JOB_PARAMS_INVALID {errors}`); **a resource pool that is not defined refuses the submit (`WORKER_UNAVAILABLE {pool}`, no row)**; the job is inserted, indexed and dispatched synchronously, so `submit` resolves with the job `running` when a slot is free. `host.ts` (`EngineHost`) routes events by proc id and holds those of an unclaimed proc until `spawn` claims it, replaying them inside `spawn` with the proc id on each event (a `hello` held before `spawn` resolved would otherwise find no proc and never get its `start`); `groupEmpty` by group; written against the Task 6 review's API too (`send` → `"ok" | "full" | "gone"`, booleans read as ok/gone; `spawned` events ignored; `JobHostExitInfo`/`JobSpawnRequest` derived from `JobHostEvent`/`JobHost.spawn`); `exitVerdict(info, answered)` maps the host's reasons to `worker.exit`'s (`timedOut` → `killed`, `noHello` → `spawnFailed`, `protocolMismatch` → `protocolError`, `killed:{cancel, recycle, idleExpired, hung}` → their own) and the job codes (resource faults are `WORKER_CRASHED {resourceFault: true}` for Task 10; crashes carry `exitCode`/`signal`/`stderrTail` in control.db only). Runner: dir and files → `prepare` when the directory is new (once per directory: retries reuse it, so `extra` stays the first attempt's) → `checkPrepared` → group (`memoryRule`: the kind's, else the family's class rule, resolved with fin-node's `resolveMemoryRule`; QoS `normal` for interactive) → spawn (`childEnv()` + `TMPDIR/TEMP/TMP` = the job's `tmp/`, cwd the job dir, grants the dir and each file) → at `hello`, the dispatched `{kind, version}` must be in `hello.kinds`, else `WORKER_PROTOCOL_MISMATCH {expected, got}` (never retried) → `start` and `watch` (stall `stallS`, timeout `timeoutS`) → after `result`/`error` the watch is cleared and a worker that has not exited 2 s later is killed with its group → at exit: open calls interrupted, `worker.exit`, the group killed (`jobEnded`, leftovers too) and closed, items and checkpoint flushed, then the outcome. Items: one transaction per second or 256 rows; checkpoints: the newest, at most one write per 10 s, flushed at the attempt's end; progress in memory, `events()` at most every 250 ms per job (trailing timer), cleared at the end. Hooks: `enterHooks` (result + `phase: hooks`) after the whole group is gone, then `onSucceeded` with `hookTimeoutS` (60 s → `JOB_TIMEOUT {hook}`), its value replacing the result; then the terminal transition, the directory deleted, `job.done`, the bus, `onDone` listeners (a throw is logged), waiters, and the next dispatch. Test-hooks fin-node (`testHooks` defined): a non-`test` family's success with `foreign > 0` fails `WORKER_CRASHED {foreign}`. **Interim policies (later tasks replace them):** a failure ends the job unless it is a worker `error` with `retry` `backoff`/`fresh` and attempts are left, which re-queues it at once (Task 10); `cancel` ends a queued job `cancelled`, returns a job in its hook phase unchanged (it ends `succeeded`) and answers a running one `WORKER_UNAVAILABLE {notYet: cancel}` (Task 9b); `pause`/`resume` `{notYet}`; `warm(family)` does nothing and warm kinds run cold (Task 11); at start the jobs root is swept except `phase: hooks` jobs' directories and queued rows are indexed, but `running` rows are only logged (Task 9b's restart) and retention does not run yet (9b); `close` stops dispatch, shuts the host down (2 s grace) and waits ≤ 5 s for runners, which then write nothing (rows stay `running` for 9b's recovery). Files: `files.ts` creates the job dir and `tmp/` 0700, directories 0700 (a writable one emptied before each attempt), sized files 0600 with `O_NOFOLLOW` (recreated before each attempt when writable or new), refuses a prepared file that is missing, a symlink, not regular or hard-linked (`IO_ERROR {file, problem}`), removes with Windows sharing-violation retries (20 × 25 ms). Calls: `CallRouter` per process (16 open, the 17th `CALL_LIMIT`, a duplicate open id `INVALID_REQUEST`, no `onCall` → `INVALID_REQUEST {method}`, a defect → `INTERNAL`, a reply that cannot be sent → an `INTERNAL` reply; `close` interrupts the open fibers and sends nothing). Tests: `apps/finisherd/test/jobs/{engine (18), runner (8), calls (6), files (9), hooks (9)}.test.ts` over `test/support/jobs.ts` (`testEngine`: temp server data, the test-hooks `fin-workers`, cgroups off, 4 cores, `killWaitMs` 2 s); the plan's list plus `an_undefined_pool_is_unavailable`, `jobs_wait_for_a_free_slot`, `get_list_and_wait_errors`, `a_throwing_on_done_listener_does_not_affect_the_job`, `protocol_mismatch_is_not_retried`, `workers_reports_caps_and_pools`, `a_crash_stores_the_stderr_tail_and_the_signal`, the exit-verdict table, `prepare_failure_fails_the_job_without_a_process`, `cancel_queued_and_running_before_9b`; `symlinked_prepared_file_is_refused` skips on Windows (symlink rights). `seventeenth_call_is_call_limit` and `open_calls_interrupted_at_exit` test the router directly (the test family makes one call at a time). Not run: Windows, macOS

a symlink or junction fails the job `JOB_FAILED {file, problem: link}`, cause `tampered`, never retried; a writable directory is emptied by `rm` of the directory itself (never follows a link) and made again; a sized file is unlinked and made with `O_EXCL | O_NOFOLLOW` (no link or hard link is ever opened or truncated); other Windows reparse points are not visible to `lstat` (sub-project 7's sandbox keeps a worker from making them). **H1** grants: the job dir `read`, `tmp/` `readWrite`, each mapped file at its own mode. **H2** the terminal `UPDATE` marks the row `phase: announce`; `job.done`, the bus, listeners and waiters go out at once, then the mark becomes `done` (one more small `UPDATE`), then the directory is deleted (a leftover is swept at start); a start re-announces `announce` rows (to the sink and the bus: `onDone` listeners attach after start and do not see them); `JobPhase` = `run | hooks | announce | done` (finished rows from before count as announced). **H3** `#run` gives the slots back and fails the job `INTERNAL` whatever throws; the attempt's exit handler always settles (`try/finally`, flushes in `finally`). **M1** `CallRouter.close()` resolves once the open calls ended and the attempt awaits it before `worker.exit`, the group's end and the outcome; - [x] Task 9b: helpers, cancel and pause, abandonment, restart and close. As built: `apps/finisherd/src/jobs/{helpers, control, restart}.ts`; engine 514 lines, runner 550. **Helpers** (`helpers.ts`): each `HelperSpec` (its id must match `[A-Za-z0-9._-]{1,64}`, and a `HelperProgram` must be registered, else `HELPER_FAILED {code: WORKER_UNAVAILABLE}`) is spawned in the job's group in turn with cwd `helpers/<id>/`, `TMPDIR` its own `tmp/`, grants the job dir `read`, its tmp `readWrite` and its files at their modes, QoS the kind's; its `hello` must be `role: helper` with its `kind` and `id`; then `attach {job, files: only its own, dirs}`; the worker spawns after every helper is attached, and `start.helpers` lists `{id, kind, files}`; a helper's `call` goes to its `HelperProgram.onCall` with `role: helper, helper: {kind, id}`; an `error` or an exit before the worker's answer fails the job `HELPER_FAILED {helper, code}` (the code from the exit verdict, or the helper's own) and kills the group (`helperFailed`); after a `result` the worker's exit is followed by `shutdown` to live helpers, ≤ 2 s, then the group is killed and its open calls end; `worker.exit` with `role: helper`, `helperKind`, `pluginId`; `RunStats.helpers` counts them for `job.done`. **Cancel and pause** (`control.ts`, `RunControl` in `runner.ts`): a queued or paused job ends `cancelled` at once; a running one: `cancel {reason: cancel}` to a started worker, the group killed after 2 s (500 ms interactive), or killed at once before the worker's `start` (helpers starting, hello pending); `cancel` resolves once the run ended; a job in its hook phase is returned unchanged. `pause {job}`: queued → `paused` (`pausedFrom: queued`, keeps `createdAt`); a running resumable job (a `resumable` kind, or a batch kind with items left) gets `cancel {reason: pause}` and 10 s, then `paused` (`pausedFrom: running`; **the stopped run is not counted: `attempts` goes back by one**); a running non-resumable job finishes. `pause {all}` stops dispatch and pauses the running resumable jobs; `resume {job}` re-queues a paused job (a `{all}` pause still holds dispatch); `resume {all}` re-queues every paused job and restarts dispatch. Requests rank cancel > close > pause; a run that succeeded first succeeds. **Abandonment**: an `abandoned` exit is recorded with the job's `io:` pools; two abandoned processes of different jobs on a pool mark it stuck (`workers().stuck`, nothing dispatched there, submit → `RESOURCE_STUCK {pool}`, exit 5); fin-jobhost's new `HostEvent::AbandonedExit {proc}` (napi `{type: "abandonedExit", proc}`, generated type) clears it when one really exits. **Restart** (`restart.ts`): sweep (keeping `phase: hooks` dirs) → re-announce → each `running` row: `phase: hooks` re-runs `onSucceeded` with `rerun: true` (an unregistered kind fails `JOB_KIND_UNKNOWN`), else `interrupted` and, when resumable with attempts left, `queued` again (the interrupted run counts), else it stays `interrupted` `JOB_INTERRUPTED` with `job.done` → queued rows indexed → retention now and every hour on the engine's clock. A batch kind's next run gets `batch.restrict(params, items not yet in job_items)` (Task 10 adds `combine`; until then a resumed batch job's result covers only its last run's items). **Close** (Decision 29): dispatch and timers stop, running `phase: run` jobs get `cancel {reason: pause}` and 2 s then their groups are killed and recovered as at a start, hook-phase jobs finish their hook, then the host shuts down (2 s); a submit while closing is `WORKER_UNAVAILABLE {reason: closing}`. `crashJobEngineForTest(engine)` (tests: nothing more is written, hooks interrupted, the host shut down at once; `TestEngine.crash()`). Tests: `helpers.test.ts` (7), `control.test.ts` (11), `restart.test.ts` (9): the plan's list plus `a_worker_that_answers_the_cancel_ends_before_the_grace`, `pause_running_non_resumable_finishes`, `a_helper_hang_is_reported_and_the_job_fails`; `cancel_running_kills_tree` and `helpers_die_with_the_job` skip on Windows (they read the pids from a file the test kind writes, the kill itself is Task 5's). Rust: `abandon_after_kill_wait` checks `AbandonedExit`. Not run: Windows, macOS

- [x] Task 10: retries, backoff, poison handling and isolation. As built: `apps/finisherd/src/jobs/isolation.ts` (pure: `classify`, `nextStep`, `backoffMs`, `isResourceFault`, the isolation state machine `startIsolation`/`afterRun`/`nextRun`/`budget`/`withoutDone`) and `retry.ts` (engine side: `runPlan`, `afterSuccess`, `afterFailure`, the `jobs.isolation` memo `{fresh?, isolation?, results?}`). Classes in Decision 31's order: resource (`SIGBUS`, i.e. an `exited` with `signal: bus`, Windows exit code `0xC0000006`, the host's `resourceFault`, an abandoned process, a crash with `resourceHealthy` false), crash (`WORKER_CRASHED` exits, protocol errors), `oom`/`oomWarm`, transient (hung, stalled, timeout, unavailable, helper, spawn, worker `backoff`), `fresh`, deterministic (worker `never`, tampered, foreign, a file `prepare` left wrong), mismatch, hook (`prepare`), cancel. Backoff `5 s × 2ⁿ` (n = attempts made), ≤ 5 min, `nextRunAt` persisted; `fresh`/`oomWarm` once at once; interactive retries at once and is never isolated. Isolation (cold batch jobs, non-interactive): runs re-queued at once with `attempts` kept (no attempt spent), state `{n, pending, inFlightAlone?, queue, poisoned, crashRuns}`; **in-flight items are a set** (several are halved, one runs alone, then the rest); a run of one item that crashes poisons it (`POISON_ITEM {item, cause}`, written to `job_items`, the rest go on); one item left of a longer run runs alone next; no item in flight halves the remainder; budget `min(2n, 2⌈log₂ n⌉ + 2p)`, past it `WORKER_CRASHED {isolation: budget, crashRuns, poisoned}`; `resourceHealthy` is asked before isolating (false or failing → a resource fault). **Every batch kind's success result is `combine(results, items)`** (default `{items: {<item>: value}, itemErrors}`), also for a single run. New test kinds: `test.fail {code, retry, failUntil}`, `test.crash how: oom`. Tests: `isolation.test.ts` (8: the classification and step tables, backoff, three fast-check models), `retries.test.ts` (21; a pause asked during a run the policy re-queues still pauses the job), `review9b.test.ts` (23), earlier suites updated for backoff (`waitThroughBackoff` in `test/support/jobs.ts`). - [x] Task 11: warm workers. As built: `apps/finisherd/src/jobs/{warm, warm-run, attempt, held-files}.ts`; `runner.ts` keeps the cold attempt over the shared `AttemptBase` (`attempt.ts`: `start`, messages, the stop, helpers, the outcome) and `attemptWith` (directory, files, `prepare`, then the worker's part). **Pool** (`warm.ts`, `WarmPools`): per family the workers, each bound to the warm key of the job it was spawned for, `spawning → ready ⇄ busy → retiring`; each has its own group (the family's interactive memory rule, also `start.limits.memoryBytes`) and directory `<jobs>/warm-<n>/` (cwd and `TMPDIR`; the hourly sweep keeps live ones), argv through `registry.program(family, job, "warm")`; `hello` must be `role: worker`, the family, `warm: true`, and each job's kind is checked against its `kinds` (`WORKER_PROTOCOL_MISMATCH`). **Hand-off**: `submitJob` calls `handOff` before any `pump()`: a warm job that heads its key's FIFO and has a ready worker is dispatched in the submit's own turn (no `schedule()` call; `Engine.schedule` is the seam tests spy on); the `start` follows once the job directory exists (p99 2.3 ms on Linux with 10,000 queued cold jobs and cold spawns cycling). Otherwise `schedule()` gets the warm pools (`schedState()`: slots, workers with `served`/`idleSince`) and its `warm`/`spawnWarm {retire?}` dispatches are applied (`dispatchQueued`, `spawnFor`). A warm dispatch takes its slots with `overrun()` (Decision 35); `Engine.dispatch(id, lease?)` and `WarmLease {run, release}` are the seam; slots go back and `pump()` runs as soon as the attempt ends (before hooks), so a ready worker never waits for `onSucceeded`. **Lifecycle**: `idleS` (60) → `shutdown`, `idleExpired`; at a `ready`, `maxJobs` (500), `maxAgeS` (1800) or a memory figure over 80 % of the group's limit spawns the replacement first, the old worker serves until its `hello`, then `shutdown` (after its current job if busy), `recycled`; a full pool retires the longest-idle ready worker of a key with no due job (`recycled`); a worker asked to go is killed after 2 s; `close` sends `shutdown` to every worker (2 s, real time) before the host goes (`done`). `warm(family)`: one worker now if the pool has none (key `""`; `JOB_KIND_UNKNOWN` for an unknown family, `WORKER_UNAVAILABLE {notWarm}` for one without a warm kind; a family whose program is a function of the job is not pre-warmed). `workers().warm`: `{family, key, pid, state, served, ageS, idleS, memory}`. **Attempt** (`warm-run.ts`): helpers in a group of their own (the kind's memory rule) → `start` → the answer → `ready` (≤ 2 s, else the worker is lost) → helpers shut down → in test-hooks builds the worker's open files and mappings under the job dir (`/proc/<pid>/fd` and `maps`; `lsof` on macOS; a rename of the dir and back on Windows) → `WORKER_CRASHED {heldFiles}` (cause `heldFiles`, deterministic) and the worker killed. Cancel: `cancel`, 500 ms, then the helper group and the worker's group killed; `JOB_CANCELLED` then `ready` within the grace keeps the worker. A crash, kill or OOM during a job loses the worker; nothing respawns until a job needs one. **Retries**: a warm job that ran before is queued with `warm.fresh` and `schedule()` gives it only a worker with `served: 0` (spawned when there is room, else a ready reused worker is retired for it); `AttemptOutcome.warmReused` reaches `classify` (`oomWarm`: once, at once); warm jobs are never isolated (`batch: false`). A warm worker that dies before its `hello` fails the attempt of its key's oldest due job (as a cold spawn would: `WORKER_UNAVAILABLE`, retried per Decision 31). Fixed: `registerTestKinds` now applies `families` overrides to `testbad` and `testplugin`. Tests: `warm.test.ts` (29 + 1 Windows-only: the plan's list plus `oom_on_a_first_job_fails`, `a_warm_worker_that_never_comes_up_fails_its_job`, two pure scheduler tests for fresh retries and `warmHead`). Not run: Windows, macOS
